# Benchmark programme — tracked state memo

Tracked copy of the unit table in `cruxvault/results/t81/PROGRAMME_STATE.md` (gitignored, per-worktree).
The gitignored file is the live one; this memo is refreshed when a phase closes. Brief:
`plan/HANDOFF_FINISH_BENCHMARK.md`. Branch: `implementation/t81-finish-benchmark` (the PI merges it to main).

**Final render at handover, 2026-09-05 17:39 PDT.** This is the state the programme is handed to the PI in,
not a close-out of every unit: four `marginal` store-truth `B_` jobs are still on Fir, and two of them
land days from now. The previous render was 201 rows at 2026-09-03 07:41 PDT (interim render 3).

## Decisions recorded 2026-09-01

| # | decision |
|---|---|
| D1 | `marginal`, `knn1`, `knn5` run once per regime; `avg`, `avg-arcsinh` once. 18 method-regime units. §12.2/§12.3 to be corrected; identity assertion for `avg`/`avg-arcsinh` to be added. |
| D2 | Build the training-residual σ pass; repoint all four rivals' `fit_sigma.py` and `avg-arcsinh` at it. |
| D3 | Truth toggle does not apply to `V_`. Exclusion recorded on the board. |
| D4 | `eic.19` band sized from a fresh measured epoch rate, `--early-stop-epochs 3`, 25-epoch ceiling. |
| D5 | The 40 `counts.h5.pre_t78p3` backups stay until every retrain is scored. |
| ops | Fir code: fresh clone `/project/def-maxwl/mforooz/CANDII_main` on this branch. Venv: `/project/def-maxwl/mforooz/EpiDenoise/candi_venv`. t90: rsync via a SLURM CPU job, checksum-verified, scratch copy left to purge. |

No CRPS is quoted: the pval arm under challenge truth carries none, the entrants being point-only. | floor.B.CANDI.eic_19 | score | 58031932+58064818 | done | /Users/mforooz/Desktop/research/libbrechteam@sfu/CANDII/cruxvault/results/t86/SEED_FLOOR.md | B macro count CRPS paired seed \|delta\| = 0.20331 (s0 2.35991 vs s1 2.56322; oracle-scaled \|delta\| 0.20911, scale_error \|delta\| 0.00580 - the split AGENTS.md 7.2 requires beside any raw CRPS, never quoted apart); n=51 experiments / 8 assays / 12 pairs, held-out chr20-22 at 6,478,903 bins, store truth, genome-wide pass, native sigma. THIS IS BY FAR THE NOISIEST OF THE THREE PANELS AND IT IS THE ONE THE GENERALIZATION CLAIM RESTS ON - fifteen times V_breadth's 0.01389 and eighteen times V_matched's 0.01149. Genome-wide scope: 0.16681 (oracle-scaled 0.11319, scale_error 0.05362). pval arm held-out: 0.00688 (pit_ks 0.01364, coverage_95 0.04551); genome-wide 0.00035 - BUT DO NOT READ THAT AS STABILITY: on the same rows the pval arm's own gwspear moves 0.17859 held-out and 0.09858 genome-wide, so a metric holding to five decimals while the ranking under it swings by a sixth of its range is not measuring the ranking; that figure is the floor for that metric only. Other held-out count \|delta\|: gwspear 0.15718, gwcorr 0.08542, ece 0.01649, c_index 0.03635, coverage_95 0.03436, auprc 0.04088, beats_marginal 0.23529, nb_nll 0.22786, mse 20.86799. THE FINDING - ON B THE SEEDS DISAGREE ABOUT RANKING, NOT SCALE, WHICH IS THE REVERSE OF V_breadth: the oracle-scaled spread 0.20911 is LARGER than the raw 0.20331 while scale_error moves only 0.00580, so removing the scale correction slightly widens the disagreement instead of shrinking it. seed_floor.py's own 'most of this is SCALE and not ranking' note is ABSENT on both B scopes, because its condition (worst track's oracle-scaled spread under half its raw spread) is false here - on the worst track the oracle-scaled spread 1.40877 exceeds the raw 1.23875. beats_marginal falling 0.68627 -> 0.45098 held-out (35 of 51 tracks vs 23) and 0.56863 -> 0.27451 genome-wide says the same thing in another currency. An oracle-scaled number offers no escape from this band. Per track held-out: median 0.19619, max 1.23875 on T_SJSA1\|B_SJSA1\|H3K4me1; genome-wide median 0.17303, max 0.63844 on the same track. The MEDIAN track moves about as much as the macro, so B's instability is NOT an averaging artefact - it is the typical track, the whole panel is noisy, and that is the reverse of the comforting reading V_breadth earned. THE NOISE-FLOOR SENTENCE: the target-clustered bootstrap floor on macro CRPS is ~0.09 with per-comparison uncertainty +/-0.13, and a seed change moves pooled imputation CRPS by 0.1195 (AGENTS.md 7.2, FROZEN - full EIC panel, production architecture, candi.eval); the 2026-08-22 amendment records 0.0608 macro CRPS under bench on the q19 smoke-scale recipe. B BREAKS THE PATTERN THE TWO V_ PANELS SET: where V_breadth (0.01389) and V_matched (0.01149) sit 6-10x BELOW the frozen figures, B's 0.20331 sits 1.7x ABOVE the 0.1195 and 2.3x ABOVE the ~0.09. On B the two recorded floors are not merely the wrong instrument, they are OPTIMISTIC, and importing either would license calls this evidence forbids. More tracks did not buy quiet - B has 51 against V_breadth's 45 - so the explanation is not population size but the task: B_ cells are the combinations the model never saw, and generalization is where a seed change shows up. Two seeds are ONE paired \|delta\|, a magnitude and not a distribution, so this figure has no sd, no confidence interval, and is NOT a target-clustered floor - the ~0.09 and the +/-0.13 stand unamended and a clustered floor on this panel is still owed. It also includes the selection rule: s0 selected epoch 14, s1 epoch 5, so it is the floor of 're-run the recipe end to end' and not of 'the same training length, different seed'. Board reading: on B, two methods differing by less than ~0.203 on macro count CRPS are the same method on this evidence, and a single-track B claim must clear ~0.196 rather than the macro. Any B-panel ranking claim already on the board must be re-checked against 0.20331 before it is repeated. Never subtract V_breadth from B (BENCHMARK_DESIGN 5.3) and never read a V_ panel's band onto B. gaussian_nll moves 45.47478 held-out and 924.31928 genome-wide (752.47761 -> 1676.79689, more than doubling) and MUST NOT back any claim. Inputs verified on Fir 2026-09-05: all four score jobs COMPLETED 0:0 (V_: 58031933 s0, 58031935 s1; B_: 58031932 s0 at 256G/66cpu ended 2026-09-04T19:21:47, 58064818 s1 at 128G/33cpu ended 2026-09-05T04:19:01), same store manifest sha256 c9a95e4e on all four, B_ pair share regime_id 7fd6c4d7 and 51 declared tracks / 12 eval pairs / 8 assays / held-out chr20-22 / eval_scope full at 121,241,684 bins, allow_missing false and no missing tracks, both B_ passes scored at kit commit 0229e56; DIFFERENT checkpoints - md5 26aeefc8 (s0) vs c8d3b3c2 (s1), each equal to its own json's manifest.ckpt_sha256 02210dfe vs 888c76ff on BOTH that seed's V_ and B_ files, which is what proves the pair is two seeds and not one checkpoint scored twice. TRAP: provenance.seed is 0 and manifest.seed is 42 in ALL FOUR jsons - neither is the training seed, which lives only in the checkpoint hash. manifest.code_sha differs (s0 0518d9d6, s1 0229e563) on both pairs, but the diff between those commits touches no file under src/candi, configs or slurm. NO POINT-ONLY GAP: sigma_source is 'track' on all 51 B_ tracks and all 45 V_ tracks of both seeds and point_only_tracks is empty in all four files, so the rivals' 48-of-51 ATAC-seq gap does not apply to CANDI - the B panel's 3 ATAC-seq tracks carry CANDI's own sigma and every CRPS here is a real distributional CRPS on both seeds. Two earlier B_ attempts (57967301 s0, 58005024 s1) died OUT_OF_MEMORY 0:125 at 32G and wrote nothing. HAZARD: backstop job 58031936 is still PENDING (Reason=Priority, 256G/66cpu) and writes the SAME path as 58064818; it was NOT cancelled - the mirrored copies under results/t86 are the durable record. Full account: sections 11, 13, 15, 16 and 19-20 of the memo. Chunk W4-floor-memo-2. | 2026-09-05 |

Killed is SIGKILL from the cgroup memory controller, the launcher's DONE line records rc=137 = 128+9, and the oom_kill line is the cgroup's own notifier; a torch allocation failure would have been a traceback and a non-137 exit. It died in pair 12/12 T_WERI-Rb-1 having written 46 of 51 tracks. MAXRSS ACROSS THE SIBLINGS, .batch step, by index -> chromosome: 0 chr1 15.99 (OOM), 1 chr2 15.99, 2 chr3 14.78, 3 chr4 14.00, 4 chr5 13.33, 5 chr6 12.57, 6 chr7 11.34, 7 chr8 11.09, 8 chr9 10.15, 9 chr10 10.20, 10 chr11 10.10, 11 chr12 9.72, 12 chr13 9.22, 14 chr15 7.63, 17 chr18 6.43, 18 chr19 4.99, 19 chr20 5.39, 20 chr21 4.18, 21 chr22 4.12; 13/15/16/22 (chr14, chr16, chr17, chrX) were still RUNNING. MaxRSS IS LINEAR IN CHROMOSOME LENGTH: least squares over the 17 completed uncapped points (chr3..chr22, lengths from CANDI_STORE/genome/chrom_sizes.json) gives 0.0688 GiB/Mb + 0.86 GiB, R2 = 0.995, which extrapolates chr1 (249.0 Mb) to 18.0 GiB and chr2 (242.2 Mb) to 17.5 GiB. So the siblings did NOT peak under 16G - chr2 sat at 15.99 G against a 16 G ceiling and completed only because it was squeezed against it, and chr1 is 2.8% longer again. --mem=32G is kept because it is 1.8x the 18.0 GiB the fit predicts, not because it is the brief's default. --time=06:00:00 is ~1.8x chr2's 3:21:03. THREE THINGS READ IN THE CODE BEFORE SUBMITTING, all true. (a) THE UNSHARDED B_ GUARD PASSES BY DESIGN: with SHARD_CHROMS unset the else branch refuses only if [ -f $OUT/manifest.json ], and there is none; it DOES NOT READ .b_once.*, which only the sharded branch does. That is the launcher's own documented hole, in its header: an UNSHARDED B_ submission over a sharded B_ root that was never merged sees no manifest and passes the guard - the sharded marker is not read on the unsharded path. This chunk exercised that hole DELIBERATELY, because the header's two prescribed alternatives are both unavailable: the merge cannot run before chr1 exists (exit 5 on the missing npz) and moving the root aside would throw away 22 chromosomes of a once-only panel. The unsharded path writes NO new marker (the : > $MARK line is gated on SHARD_CHROMS=1), so .b_once.57953581 stays the sole marker and the merge still finds it. (b) THE DUMP OVERWRITES A PARTIAL npz CLEANLY: src/candi/bench/dump.py:192-194 writes path.name + .tmp.npz with np.savez_compressed then os.replace onto the final path - an atomic rename, so the 46 partial chr1.npz are complete files that get replaced, not appended to, and NOTHING NEEDED DELETING. (c) THE MERGE TOLERATES A PRE-EXISTING manifest.json AND STILL FINDS THE TEMPLATES: it globs .shard_manifest.*.json and takes shards[0] as template, then writes manifest.json.merge.tmp and os.replace's it onto manifest.json; its guard branch is the SHARDED one (SHARD_CHROMS=1 is mandatory with SHARD_MERGE=1 else exit 6) where MARK=$OUT/.b_once.57953581 exists, so both refusal clauses are false. THE BOARD MUST SAY WHICH ROOT AND WHICH DEVICE A CANDI ROW CAME FROM: three prediction roots per regime now carry method: CANDI in their manifest - the canonical GPU V_, the CPU V_cpu, and CANDI_s1 - because the launcher has no METHOD variable and --method CANDI is a literal, so entrant identity lives in the PATHS. THIS IS THE RELAUNCH of the four seed-0 units chunk W3-candi-cpu submitted at 04:30 on 2026-09-03 and lost, 92 tasks out of 92, to two launcher defects: Defect 1, the positional sed -n 1p/2p/3p capture of $PLAN off a stdout the library also writes to; Defect 2, the non-atomic derived-regime write that 12 concurrent shards raced on. K16 fixes both - the plan heredoc now runs under contextlib.redirect_stdout(sys.stderr) and emits PLAN:chroms= / PLAN:n_tracks= / PLAN:want_time= markers that bash greps for by NAME, so position is no longer a contract, and tools/declare_eval_pairs.py split writes tmp + os.replace. CPU-SHARDED MODE: DEVICE=cpu SHARD_CHROMS=1, --array=0-22%12 --gres=none --cpus-per-task=8 --mem=16G --time=1-00:00:00 (cpubase_bycore_b3), then a SHARD_MERGE=1 SHARD_ARRAY=<array id> job afterok on the array (--gres=none --cpus-per-task=1 --mem=8G --time=00:20:00) that verifies every declared track x planned chromosome holds an npz and writes the ONE manifest.json, adding device= and sharded: true with the array id to notes. Scorers chain afterok on the MERGE, never on the array, because a one-chromosome manifest can sit in the root mid-array and the scorer reads manifest.chroms. Every variable is exported in the shell and every submit passes bare --export=ALL (a comma-valued variable inside --export=ALL,VAR=a,b,c arrives truncated at the first comma). ALL 20 SUBMISSIONS WENT IN FROM ONE ssh SHELL, so every dependency was taken against a live job id captured in that shell; scontrol confirmed all 20 dependencies landed intact, including the two two-clause ones. sbatch --test-only was run once per distinct shape (array / merge / genome-wide score / held-out challenge score) and all four validated; --gres=none is accepted and overrides the script's #SBATCH --gres. The four seed-0 arrays are staggered --begin now / +10 / +20 / +30 minutes, because more than 12 simultaneous torch imports off the shared /project venv fail with partial-module ImportErrors; %12 caps one array and the stagger keeps the four first bursts from coinciding. The two seed-1 arrays are chained --dependency=afterany on the seed-0 eic_19 array of the SAME panel, so at most four arrays ever run at once. THE BOARD MUST SAY WHICH ROOT AND WHICH DEVICE A CANDI ROW CAME FROM: three prediction roots per regime now carry method: CANDI in their manifest - the canonical GPU V_, the CPU V_cpu, and CANDI_s1 - because the launcher has no METHOD variable and --method CANDI is a literal, so entrant identity lives in the PATHS. THIS IS THE RELAUNCH of the four seed-0 units chunk W3-candi-cpu submitted at 04:30 on 2026-09-03 and lost, 92 tasks out of 92, to two launcher defects: Defect 1, the positional sed -n 1p/2p/3p capture of $PLAN off a stdout the library also writes to; Defect 2, the non-atomic derived-regime write that 12 concurrent shards raced on. K16 fixes both - the plan heredoc now runs under contextlib.redirect_stdout(sys.stderr) and emits PLAN:chroms= / PLAN:n_tracks= / PLAN:want_time= markers that bash greps for by NAME, so position is no longer a contract, and tools/declare_eval_pairs.py split writes tmp + os.replace. CPU-SHARDED MODE: DEVICE=cpu SHARD_CHROMS=1, --array=0-22%12 --gres=none --cpus-per-task=8 --mem=16G --time=1-00:00:00 (cpubase_bycore_b3), then a SHARD_MERGE=1 SHARD_ARRAY=<array id> job afterok on the array (--gres=none --cpus-per-task=1 --mem=8G --time=00:20:00) that verifies every declared track x planned chromosome holds an npz and writes the ONE manifest.json, adding device= and sharded: true with the array id to notes. Scorers chain afterok on the MERGE, never on the array, because a one-chromosome manifest can sit in the root mid-array and the scorer reads manifest.chroms. Every variable is exported in the shell and every submit passes bare --export=ALL (a comma-valued variable inside --export=ALL,VAR=a,b,c arrives truncated at the first comma). ALL 20 SUBMISSIONS WENT IN FROM ONE ssh SHELL, so every dependency was taken against a live job id captured in that shell; scontrol confirmed all 20 dependencies landed intact, including the two two-clause ones. sbatch --test-only was run once per distinct shape (array / merge / genome-wide score / held-out challenge score) and all four validated; --gres=none is accepted and overrides the script's #SBATCH --gres. The four seed-0 arrays are staggered --begin now / +10 / +20 / +30 minutes, because more than 12 simultaneous torch imports off the shared /project venv fail with partial-module ImportErrors; %12 caps one array and the stagger keeps the four first bursts from coinciding. The two seed-1 arrays are chained --dependency=afterany on the seed-0 eic_19 array of the SAME panel, so at most four arrays ever run at once. THE BOARD MUST SAY WHICH ROOT AND WHICH DEVICE A CANDI ROW CAME FROM: three prediction roots per regime now carry method: CANDI in their manifest - the canonical GPU V_, the CPU V_cpu, and CANDI_s1 - because the launcher has no METHOD variable and --method CANDI is a literal, so entrant identity lives in the PATHS. No scancel was run by this chunk; no root, checkpoint or json was deleted or overwritten; no tracked file was edited; no git commit was made. | 2026-09-03 |

Same 12-pair / 51-track B_ shape as the pass that died at pair 4/12; this job is at pair 1/12. WHY IT IS SAFE TO RUN BOTH ON ONE OUT PATH: the scorer writes its json ONCE, at the very end of score_external, after every pair is scored - there is no incremental write to race on. So whichever pass finishes first writes the file, and if the other also finishes it overwrites hours later with content derived from the same prediction root, the same regime json and the same store truth - identical by construction. The cost of the duplicate is CPU time; the cost of not submitting it is another 6-8 hours lost to an OOM that is already visible in sstat. The B_ memory was set from the B_ death: 256G with --cpus-per-task=66. NEW LINE: --gres=none --cpus-per-task=66 --mem=256G --time=60:00:00, no --dependency. OOM DIAGNOSIS AND RESUBMISSION by chunk W3-candi-score-oom. The score pass was submitted with the script default --mem=32G --cpus-per-task=4 (slurm/t81_score_external.sh lines 51-53); the K16 relaunch line passed neither --mem nor --cpus-per-task, so 32G was never a chosen number. SCALING, read in src/candi/bench/external.py: score_external walks pair by pair; for each pair stream_truth holds truth for EVERY track of that pair at once and is only freed by `del truth` after the pair; inside it read_track_arrays(root/dirname, chroms, n_bins) concatenates ALL 23 chromosomes of ONE track into memory at once, one float32 vector per array PRESENT in the npz, and build_record then makes a second cut() copy of each, so pred and rec are live together. Genome-wide is ~121 M bins x 4 B = ~485 MB PER ARRAY. CANDI writes FIVE arrays (PRED_ARRAYS = signal_mu, signal_sigma, mu, n, peak_score) where a pval-only baseline writes ONE, so a CANDI track costs ~5 x 485 MB = 2.4 GB to load and ~2.4 GB again to cut = ~4.8 GB, against ~1 GB for a baseline track - the ~2x per-track factor the brief names, and more like 5x against a one-array rival. On top of that SCOPE=genomewide sets split, so score_track runs TWICE per track (held-out chr20-22 and full genome), each taking its own top-1% sort/argsort over a 121 M-element vector (an int64 argsort alone is ~968 MB). The accumulator across pairs is small and is NOT the driver: _binarise (harness.py:1700) returns np.packbits output, ~15 MB per array, ~30 MB per track, so `binarised` reaches only ~1.5 GB over 51 tracks, and per_track/per_track_gw hold scalars. THE PEAK THEREFORE SCALES AS (tracks in the fattest pair) x 485 MB x arrays-per-track, plus the per-track load+cut+argsort transient, and it is reached LATE - which is why both deaths came hours in and well before the last pair. Baseline genome-wide passes were submitted at --mem=96G (cruxvault/results/t81/SCORES_BASELINES_B.md); the CANDI passes inherited 32G. PARTITION: sbatch --test-only showed --mem=128G and --mem=256G at the script's 4 CPUs BOTH route to cpularge_bycore_b4 on the scarce fb* large-memory nodes with a projected start of 2026-09-10 - a week out. cpubase_bycore_b4 reports MaxMemPerNode=UNLIMITED, so there is NO per-job bycore ceiling to exceed; what routes a job off cpubase is the PER-CORE memory rule, 768000 MB / 192 cores = 4000 MB per core. Scaling --cpus-per-task to that rule (33 CPUs for 128G, 66 for 256G) keeps the job on cpubase_bycore_b4 and moved the projected start to 2026-09-04T00:36 - six days earlier. That is the route taken: cpubase with CPUs scaled to the per-core rule, NOT the bynode partition. The extra cores are bought for their memory; the scorer is single-threaded numpy. No scancel was run - the running originals were left alone; nothing was deleted or overwritten; no tracked file was edited; no git commit was made. The 60 h (b4) genome-wide pass hit its wall; MaxRSS on the batch step was 37679228K = 35.9 GiB against ReqMem 96G (37 %, AveRSS == MaxRSS), so walltime was the only failure and memory was never near it. Rescued in TWO steps, both --array=4 (single index, accepted; no 4-4 needed) off METHOD_LIST=(avg avg-arcsinh knn1 knn5 marginal), both --gres=none (the script MIG default routes a CPU-only job to gpubase_bygpu, priced 20 days worse), both with no --mem so the script's #SBATCH --mem=96G applies as on the original, and TRUTH/SIGMA/CRPS_APPROX/VARPOOL all unset - store truth, no sigma table, EXACT closed-form count arm, unchanged from the timed-out pass. REGIME/PANEL/SCOPE exported in the shell and passed with a BARE --export=ALL (never --export=ALL,VAR=... which truncates at the first comma). sbatch --test-only priced both shapes first, rc=0, neither refused for a maintenance reservation: 12:00:00 -> cpubase_bycore_b2 start 2026-09-06T02:10:42 on fc30615; 5-18:00:00 -> cpubase_bycore_b5 start 2026-09-06T19:23:42 on fc30118. STEP 1 heldout SCOPE=heldout --time=12:00:00: chr20+21+22 only and NO --held-out-chroms (launcher: heldout) CHROMS=$HELD_OUT; HELD_ARG=""), so its json carries ONE aggregation and NO genome_wide block; provenance.scope records which, so an absent block is never ambiguous. Held-out is ~1/19 of the store, NOT ~1/40: the launcher header anchors chr21 at 1.87 M bins against 121 M for the whole store, and chr20+21+22 is 161.97 Mb at 25 bp = 6.48 M bins, so 121/6.48 = 18.7x. STEP 2 genomewide SCOPE=genomewide --time=5-18:00:00 (b5, 138 h): the SAME pass that timed out, in the band SCORES_BASELINES_EIC19.md 6 had already named - it recorded that the 60 h/b4 band came from t81_score_external.sh's pinned interface, disagreed with t49_baselines_score.sh's own --time=5-18:00:00 example, and said a TIMEOUT after 2-12:00:00 means the resubmit wants b5. NO --dependency clause, by design. BOTH STEPS WRITE THE SAME OUT: OUT=$SCORES_ROOT/$METHOD/$REGNAME/$TRUTH.$PANEL.json does not depend on SCOPE, so the genome-wide json lands days later and OVERWRITES the held-out one, replacing a one-aggregation file with held-out macro/panels PLUS a parallel genome_wide block. That is the intended end state, not a collision. THE COST: fill-panels MUST BE RE-RUN after the genome-wide json lands. This method's store.V_.json was checked and already carries a genome_wide block (provenance.scope.genome_wide_computed true), so off the held-out store.B_.json fill-panels can fill the HELD-OUT V_matched cell and that cell only (the genome-wide V_matched has no B_ side to pair with yet); once the genome-wide store.B_.json replaces it, the genome-wide V_matched becomes fillable AND the held-out fill must be re-run, because the json it read no longer exists. WHY IT IS SAFE TO RUN BOTH ON ONE OUT PATH: the scorer writes its json ONCE, at the very end of score_external, after every pair is scored - there is no incremental write to race on. OOM DIAGNOSIS AND RESUBMISSION by chunk W3-candi-score-oom. The score pass was submitted with the script default --mem=32G --cpus-per-task=4 (slurm/t81_score_external.sh lines 51-53); the K16 relaunch line passed neither --mem nor --cpus-per-task, so 32G was never a chosen number. SCALING, read in src/candi/bench/external.py: score_external walks pair by pair; for each pair stream_truth holds truth for EVERY track of that pair at once and is only freed by `del truth` after the pair; inside it read_track_arrays(root/dirname, chroms, n_bins) concatenates ALL 23 chromosomes of ONE track into memory at once, one float32 vector per array PRESENT in the npz, and build_record then makes a second cut() copy of each, so pred and rec are live together. Genome-wide is ~121 M bins x 4 B = ~485 MB PER ARRAY. CANDI writes FIVE arrays (PRED_ARRAYS = signal_mu, signal_sigma, mu, n, peak_score) where a pval-only baseline writes ONE, so a CANDI track costs ~5 x 485 MB = 2.4 GB to load and ~2.4 GB again to cut = ~4.8 GB, against ~1 GB for a baseline track - the ~2x per-track factor the brief names, and more like 5x against a one-array rival. On top of that SCOPE=genomewide sets split, so score_track runs TWICE per track (held-out chr20-22 and full genome), each taking its own top-1% sort/argsort over a 121 M-element vector (an int64 argsort alone is ~968 MB). The accumulator across pairs is small and is NOT the driver: _binarise (harness.py:1700) returns np.packbits output, ~15 MB per array, ~30 MB per track, so `binarised` reaches only ~1.5 GB over 51 tracks, and per_track/per_track_gw hold scalars. THE PEAK THEREFORE SCALES AS (tracks in the fattest pair) x 485 MB x arrays-per-track, plus the per-track load+cut+argsort transient, and it is reached LATE - which is why both deaths came hours in and well before the last pair. Baseline genome-wide passes were submitted at --mem=96G (cruxvault/results/t81/SCORES_BASELINES_B.md); the CANDI passes inherited 32G. PARTITION: sbatch --test-only showed --mem=128G and --mem=256G at the script's 4 CPUs BOTH route to cpularge_bycore_b4 on the scarce fb* large-memory nodes with a projected start of 2026-09-10 - a week out. cpubase_bycore_b4 reports MaxMemPerNode=UNLIMITED, so there is NO per-job bycore ceiling to exceed; what routes a job off cpubase is the PER-CORE memory rule, 768000 MB / 192 cores = 4000 MB per core. Baseline genome-wide passes were submitted at --mem=96G (cruxvault/results/t81/SCORES_BASELINES_B.md); the CANDI passes inherited 32G. PARTITION: sbatch --test-only showed --mem=128G and --mem=256G at the script's 4 CPUs BOTH route to cpularge_bycore_b4 on the scarce fb* large-memory nodes with a projected start of 2026-09-10 - a week out. cpubase_bycore_b4 reports MaxMemPerNode=UNLIMITED, so there is NO per-job bycore ceiling to exceed; what routes a job off cpubase is the PER-CORE memory rule, 768000 MB / 192 cores = 4000 MB per core. Scaling --cpus-per-task to that rule (33 CPUs for 128G, 66 for 256G) keeps the job on cpubase_bycore_b4 and moved the projected start to 2026-09-04T00:36 - six days earlier. That is the route taken: cpubase with CPUs scaled to the per-core rule, NOT the bynode partition. The extra cores are bought for their memory; the scorer is single-threaded numpy. No scancel was run - the running originals were left alone; nothing was deleted or overwritten; no tracked file was edited; no git commit was made. Full account: the '## Score-pass OOMs and resubmissions (2026-09-03)' section of cruxvault/results/t81/TRAIN_CANDI_EIC19.md. \|\|\| PRIOR ROW (job 57953594): RESUBMITTED 2026-09-03 by chunk W3-candi-cpu-2 on the CPU route, superseding nothing - the queued GPU sibling 57943015 was NOT touched and still chains on the GPU predict 57943014. TRUTH=store SCOPE=genomewide. REGIME=/scratch/mforooz/t81_pred/CANDI_s1/eic_19/regime.eic_19.V_.json, PRED=/scratch/mforooz/t81_pred/CANDI_s1/eic_19/V_, OUT=/project/def-maxwl/mforooz/t81_scores/CANDI_s1/eic_19/store.V_.json, SCOPE=genomewide HELD_OUT_CHROMS=chr20,chr21,chr22; --gres=none --time=60:00:00 --dependency=afterok:57953593, the MERGE. Band cpubase_bycore_b4, --mem=32G --cpus-per-task=4. No SIGMA, no challenge pass. /project/def-maxwl/mforooz/t81_scores/ carries no CANDI_s1/ directory at all, so the target json is absent and so is its parent. THE FLOOR PASS, when both sides exist: tools/seed_floor.py reads a whole panel block and one panel's spread is not another's, so it runs three times per scope - --panel V_breadth, --panel V_matched, --panel B - with S0=/project/def-maxwl/mforooz/t81_scores/CANDI/eic_19 and S1=/project/def-maxwl/mforooz/t81_scores/CANDI_s1/eic_19; add --scope genome-wide to read the genome_wide block. STATE queued: PENDING (Dependency) all sitting. Kit KIT=/project/def-maxwl/mforooz/CANDII_main at HEAD 0518d9d (K16: CANDI predict plan is read by marker with library stdout redirected; declare_eval_pairs split writes atomically); grep -c PLAN:chroms slurm/t81_predict_candi.sh = 3, so K16 is on the kit and no pull was needed. THIS IS THE RELAUNCH of the four seed-0 units chunk W3-candi-cpu submitted at 04:30 on 2026-09-03 and lost, 92 tasks out of 92, to two launcher defects: Defect 1, the positional sed -n 1p/2p/3p capture of $PLAN off a stdout the library also writes to; Defect 2, the non-atomic derived-regime write that 12 concurrent shards raced on. K16 fixes both - the plan heredoc now runs under contextlib.redirect_stdout(sys.stderr) and emits PLAN:chroms= / PLAN:n_tracks= / PLAN:want_time= markers that bash greps for by NAME, so position is no longer a contract, and tools/declare_eval_pairs.py split writes tmp + os.replace. CPU-SHARDED MODE: DEVICE=cpu SHARD_CHROMS=1, --array=0-22%12 --gres=none --cpus-per-task=8 --mem=16G --time=1-00:00:00 (cpubase_bycore_b3), then a SHARD_MERGE=1 SHARD_ARRAY=<array id> job afterok on the array (--gres=none --cpus-per-task=1 --mem=8G --time=00:20:00) that verifies every declared track x planned chromosome holds an npz and writes the ONE manifest.json, adding device= and sharded: true with the array id to notes. Scorers chain afterok on the MERGE, never on the array, because a one-chromosome manifest can sit in the root mid-array and the scorer reads manifest.chroms. Every variable is exported in the shell and every submit passes bare --export=ALL (a comma-valued variable inside --export=ALL,VAR=a,b,c arrives truncated at the first comma). ALL 20 SUBMISSIONS WENT IN FROM ONE ssh SHELL, so every dependency was taken against a live job id captured in that shell; scontrol confirmed all 20 dependencies landed intact, including the two two-clause ones. sbatch --test-only was run once per distinct shape (array / merge / genome-wide score / held-out challenge score) and all four validated; --gres=none is accepted and overrides the script's #SBATCH --gres. The four seed-0 arrays are staggered --begin now / +10 / +20 / +30 minutes, because more than 12 simultaneous torch imports off the shared /project venv fail with partial-module ImportErrors; %12 caps one array and the stagger keeps the four first bursts from coinciding. The two seed-1 arrays are chained --dependency=afterany on the seed-0 eic_19 array of the SAME panel, so at most four arrays ever run at once. THE BOARD MUST SAY WHICH ROOT AND WHICH DEVICE A CANDI ROW CAME FROM: three prediction roots per regime now carry method: CANDI in their manifest - the canonical GPU V_, the CPU V_cpu, and CANDI_s1 - because the launcher has no METHOD variable and --method CANDI is a literal, so entrant identity lives in the PATHS. No scancel was run by this chunk; no root, checkpoint or json was deleted or overwritten; no tracked file was edited; no git commit was made.  | 2026-09-05 |

Each line names a row this render copied as written, and the evidence that disagrees with it.
No row was edited — this chunk does not own the rows. 23 rows are not `done`, and all 23 appear
below: for 21 of them an artefact or a log entry contradicts the `state` itself, and the last
two are current in every column except `date`.

- `train.Avocado.eic_19.s0`, `train.Avocado.eic_pilot.s0` — rows say `running`, last written
  2026-09-02. Avocado has since finished its σ pass, both `V_` passes and both `B_` passes, and
  every addressable Avocado cell is stamped on both boards, so the training they depend on

## Still running at handover

Four Fir jobs, all of them the `marginal` store-truth `B_` score, plus one original left
running on purpose. Nothing else in the programme is in flight. The rescue is written up in
`cruxvault/results/t81/SCORES_BASELINES_B.md`, section `marginal store B_ rescue (2026-09-05)`.

| job | unit | what it yields | when |
|---|---|---|---|
| 58260217_4 | `score.store.B_.marginal.eic_19` — held-out only (`SCOPE=heldout`, 12 h b2 band) | the held-out `store.B_` cell and the held-out `store.V_matched` cell on `eic.19` | RUNNING; ~6 h projected, so tonight |
| 58260219_4 | `score.store.B_.marginal.eic_pilot` — held-out only (12 h b2 band) | the same two held-out cells on `eic.pilot` | RUNNING; ~9.4 h projected, so overnight |
| 58260218_4 | `score.store.B_.marginal.eic_19` — genome-wide (`SCOPE=genomewide`, 5-18:00:00 b5, 138 h) | the genome-wide `store.B_` and `store.V_matched` cells on `eic.19` | PENDING; ~113 h projected at 7.27 h/pair, so ≈ 2026-09-09 |
| 58260220_4 | `score.store.B_.marginal.eic_pilot` — genome-wide (b5, 138 h) | the genome-wide `store.B_` and `store.V_matched` cells on `eic.pilot` | PENDING; ~174 h projected at 9.38 h/pair — over the 138 h request and over b5's 168 h ceiling, so it may not finish |

Both steps of a regime write the **same** path, `.../marginal/<regime>/store.B_.json`: the
genome-wide json lands days later and overwrites the held-out one, replacing a one-aggregation
file with held-out macro and panels **plus** a genome-wide block. That is the intended end
state, not a collision — but it means `fill-panels` must be re-run for that method after each
genome-wide json lands, because the json the held-out fill read no longer exists.

The pilot original `57918113_4` was deliberately **not** cancelled and is still running; its own
wall falls ≈ 2026-09-06 15:00, around pair 6 of 12, so it was never going to finish. If it did,
it would write the same json with both blocks, which is what 58260220_4 is for.

The `eic_19` original `57911697_4` TIMEOUT'd at 2026-09-05 17:01:39 after 2-12:00:14 against a
2-12:00:00 limit, at pair 8 of 12. `MaxRSS` on its batch step was 35.9 GiB against `ReqMem` 96G,
so walltime was the only failure and memory was never near it.

## Programme-wide caveats

Facts about how these runs were produced, not judgements about what they show. Each one names
where it is on the record.

- **No number on this page is a result, and ranking is off on purpose.** Every board view still
  compiles `unranked`, because `noise_floor.measured` is `false` on both boards. That flag is
  now **deliberately** false, not merely unset: the seed floor **is** measured (t86,
  `cruxvault/results/t86/SEED_FLOOR.md`), and `tools/leaderboard.py:300-302` shows that setting
  it `true` turns ranking **on** for ranked panels and scopes. Turning ranking on is the
  experiment-lane merge gate, so it is the PI's flip and not an agent's. The `number` column is
  an address into a run, not a score to quote.
- **The measured seed floor, on the three panels it covers.** Two seeds of the same CANDI recipe
  on `eic_19` (seed 0 and seed 1, same passes) give **one paired |Δ| per metric** — no sd, no
  CI, no significance. Held-out is the ranked scope, so these are the held-out figures, and no
  CRPS is quoted without its `oracle_scaled` / `scale_error` split (`AGENTS.md` §7.2 rule 2):

  | panel | count-arm macro CRPS \|Δ\| | of which `oracle_scaled` | of which `scale_error` |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | `V_breadth` | 0.0139 | 0.0108 | 0.0031 |
  | `V_matched` | 0.0115 | 0.0094 | 0.0020 |
  | `B` | 0.2033 | 0.2091 | 0.0058 |

  **The sentence that must travel with every one of them.** `AGENTS.md` §7.2's frozen references
  stand unamended: a **target-clustered noise floor on macro CRPS of ~0.09**, and a **0.1195**
  pooled-CRPS shift from a **seed change alone**. The three numbers above are seed-change |Δ|
  values, **not** clustered floors — a clustered bootstrap asks how much the number moves when
  the *targets* are resampled, a different and generally larger question — so none of them may
  ever be presented as a clustered floor, and a proper clustered floor on all three panels is
  still owed. On `B` the `oracle_scaled` spread is *larger* than the raw spread while
  `scale_error` barely moves: the two seeds disagree about **ranking** on the blind panel, not
  about scale. `B` sits above both frozen references; the two `V_` panels sit well below them.
- **CANDI is scored on every cell the design allows.** Both regimes and the seed-1 lineage
  finished their store-truth `B_` passes (2026-09-04 19:21, 2026-09-05 02:42, 2026-09-05 04:18)
  after their `V_` passes had landed on 2026-09-04. Nothing CANDI is pending. The board stands
  at **164 rows** after 22 stamp passes; every `add` is recorded in `cruxvault/results/t81/BOARD.md`.
- **The 32 GB scorer OOM, and what it cost.** Six CANDI score passes were submitted at the
  baselines' shape and every one of them was killed OUT_OF_MEMORY partway through its pair list
  — the pilot `V_` at pair 16 of 26 and the `eic_19` `B_` at pair 4 of 12 on 2026-09-03, the two
  seed-1 passes the same way overnight. Live `MaxRSS` on the survivors peaked near 37–41 GB,
  past the 32 G point where the others died. All six were resubmitted with more memory: three
  `V_` passes and one `B_` pass at 256G (58031931–58031933, 58031935, 58031932), and — when the
  two remaining 256G `B_` copies were estimated to start a **day** later — two 128G duplicates
  (58064817 pilot, 58064818 seed-1) that started within the hour and wrote the jsons. The
  redundant 256G backstops 58031934 and 58031936 were cancelled with the PI's one-time
  permission on 2026-09-05, after their jsons had already landed via the 128G copies.
- **The CANDI CPU roots are canonical — the PI ruled, and the GPU submissions are gone.** CANDI
  predictions ran on CPU, sharded by chromosome, because the `def-maxwl_gpu` pool gave no start
  estimate for the better part of a day. The CPU `V_` passes wrote
  `/scratch/mforooz/t81_pred/CANDI/<regime>/V_cpu` rather than a root a queued GPU job might
  still claim, and the CPU `B_` passes wrote the canonical
  `/project/def-maxwl/mforooz/t81_pred_B/CANDI/<regime>/B_`. Those `V_cpu` roots are now the
  **only** CANDI `V_` roots: every doomed GPU predict has left the queue without writing, so
  there is no second root and no ambiguity. Seed 1 writes its own `CANDI_s1` roots and touches
  neither. Measured shard wall time was 1:15–1:35 h per chromosome at 8 cores.
- **The seed-1 CANDI run is a floor measurement, never a board row.** `train.CANDI.eic_19.s1`
  (57865895_0) COMPLETED in 13:45:29, best epoch 5, early stop at epoch 11, staged at
  `/project/def-maxwl/mforooz/t81_checkpoints/t81_eic_19_s1.{best.ckpt,json}`. It exists to size
  the seed-to-seed spread (`cruxvault/results/t86/RUN.md`), and it is the second seed behind the
  three floor numbers above. One CANDI row per regime on the board.
  **A trap for anyone reading the jsons:** `provenance.seed` (0) and `manifest.seed` (42) are
  **not** the training seed in either file — the checkpoint hash is the seed's fingerprint.
- **Three chromosome shards were lost to the 16 G shard limit and repaired one at a time.** chr1
  is the largest shard and it OOM'd on `eic_19` `B_`, on `eic_pilot` `B_` and on seed-1 `B_`
  (which also lost chr12 to a separate FAILED task). Each was re-predicted as an unsharded
  single-chromosome run at 32G into the same root, and a fresh `SHARD_MERGE` was re-chained
  behind it. Every merged manifest names 23 chromosomes.
- **Three launcher defects were found on the night of 2026-09-02/03. All three are fixed,
  tested and merged** — one in chunk K14, two in chunk K16, with the CPU-shard chunk K15 between
  them. The CANDI predict launcher had **never run end-to-end** before that night, so every
  CANDI predict submitted before the last fix was dead at its first step and wrote nothing — no
  root, no marker, no npz, no json, and no `B_` once-budget spent.
  - **K14** — `slurm/t81_predict_candi.sh` asked for `CANDI_STORE/eic/genome/chrom_sizes.json`,
    which does not exist: the genome layer is `CANDI_STORE/genome`. This is the same path bug
    K13 had already fixed in the scorer. With the chromosome list empty the PLAN step exited 1
    at start. The fix takes the list from the corpus genome directory intersected with what the
    store actually holds (`CorpusStore.n_bins()`): the corpus manifest lists 23 chromosomes, the
    shared genome layer 24, and the store holds no chrY. Merged as `b506bd3`.
  - **K15** — the CPU-sharded mode above. Not itself a defect fix; it made the K14-class failure
    exit early and legibly instead of running garbage.
  - **K16, defect one** — `harness.py:701` prints its `[bench] N declared eval pair(s)` banner to
    **stdout**, and the launcher captured the PLAN heredoc's stdout positionally, so the
    chromosome list became that banner. Present since chunk C (`1633756`). The fix puts the PLAN
    body under `redirect_stdout(stderr)` and emits its values as `PLAN:<name>=` lines the
    launcher reads by marker.
  - **K16, defect two** — `tools/declare_eval_pairs.py:316-317` wrote the derived regime file
    non-atomically and then re-read it, and 12 shards shared one `$WORKSPACE/regime.<r>.<P>.json`.
    The fix writes through `mkstemp` + `os.replace`. Merged as `0518d9d`.
  - What the defects cost: 6 GPU predicts (57910740, 57914202, 57943014, 57943025, 57944437,
    57944438, plus the chained 57942777/57942786/57943015/57943029) and 92 CPU shard tasks
    (86 exit 6, 6 exit 1). SLURM dropped 10 dependents itself as `DependencyNeverSatisfied`.
- **Avocado ran on two devices, and that is recorded on both boards.** `V_` predictions ran on
  GPU under bf16 autocast; `B_` predictions ran on CPU in fp32, because `autocast` self-disables
  on CPU (`competitors/avocado/predict.py:133`, and its device fall-back at `:71`). The device
  note is `cruxvault/results/t81/W3_AVOCADO.md` §26. The CPU `B_` pass cost **13–19 min per
  chromosome** per regime at 8 cores — it needs chr20-22 only — against a GPU pair that had been
  pending for hours. Avocado is complete: all five addressable cells on both boards.
- **CANDI's prediction roots are the only compressed ones.** Measured off the roots themselves:
  CANDI's five-array `npz` files deflate **1.177×** (`B_`) and **1.167×** (`V_`), against
  baselines at **1.000×** (`np.savez`, uncompressed) and eDICE at **1.208×**. Recorded in
  `cruxvault/results/t81/TRAIN_CANDI_EIC19.md` and written into `plan/BENCHMARK_DESIGN.md`
  §12.6 by docs pass 2. A side finding on the same read: the manifest `arms` list omits
  `peak_score` (an undercount no reader depends on), and the shard array shares one log file
  because the launcher's `--output` carries no `%a`.
- **One effective code state for the jobs queued across the fixes.** Jobs submitted while the
  Fir kit was at `bd71b25` banner a newer sha when they start. The commits in between are
  leaderboard rows plus the K14 launcher fix, and neither changes any prediction or scoring code
  path those jobs run, so the queue is one effective code state, not two.
- **Two baselines have no count arm.** `avg-arcsinh` and Avocado predict `-log10 p` only, so
  `macro.count` is empty and their fills needed `--allow-missing`; the ATAC-seq targets are
  point-only for the same class of reason. Recorded as caveats on both boards.
- **The D1 collapse is a fact about the eic.pilot board, not a bug.** Under decision D1 the
  `avg` and `avg-arcsinh` rows on `eic.pilot` are the `eic_19` generation root scored at pilot
  addresses; only `avg-arcsinh` names a σ table (`sigma.eic_19.json`) — `avg` fits none. The
  stamp-22 pass confirmed the collapsed `avg` pilot `B_` and `V_matched` blocks are bit-identical
  to their `eic.19` counterparts. A factual caveat to that effect stands on `eic.pilot`.
- **`marginal` predicts a constant per track**, so its per-track Pearson and Spearman are `NaN`
  by construction. That is a property of the baseline, not a failure, and the `gwcorr`/`gwspear`
  drop is carried as a caveat wherever a `marginal` row appears.
- **The Avocado score launcher has a race, and it was worked around, not fixed.** Its
  `score.sh` writes its derived `PANEL_REGIME` file non-atomically, so two same-regime score
  jobs can collide; the store pass was serialised behind the challenge pass with `afterany`
  instead. Left on record for a docs pass.
- **The figure is a placement, never a board row.** `cruxvault/results/t81/CANDI_IN_2019_FIELD.md`
  and `.svg` put CANDI into the 25-entrant 2019 anchor field under challenge truth on the `B_`
  panel. Stage 2 of the challenge ranker ranks across methods within a cell, so adding CANDI
  changes every entrant's rank denominator — and there is **no floor on the capped-rank-fraction
  axis**, because the t86 floor is measured on CRPS, not on placement. Read it as a placement
  and not as a score. A pilot probe was run and deliberately **not** published: it is a separate
  ranking and cannot share the `eic.19` axis.
- **The programme's crux tasks are closed and the vault lints clean.** t77, t81, t86, t87, t90
  and t91 are `done` with resolving outputs; t47's bare URL was replaced by
  `cruxvault/results/t47/LINK.md`; `crux validate --check=tree,tasks` exits 0. Deliverables and
  `FIR_PATH.txt` are written as gitignored evidence under `cruxvault/results/`. None of the six
  carries hypothesis refs, so none was closed with a `--concluded` verdict, and no task was
  accepted — `crux task accept` is the PI's signature.
- **The design document was rewritten from measurement, not from plan.** Docs pass 2 rewrote
  `plan/BENCHMARK_DESIGN.md` §15 as of 2026-09-05, filled §12.6 from the measured ratios above,
  put every CRPS in §12.9 in a table beside its `oracle_scaled` / `scale_error` split, and added
  a second batch to the `plan/HANDOFF_FINISH_BENCHMARK.md` appendix of what the handoff had wrong.

## What the PI decides

Four open items. None of them is an agent's call, and none was pre-empted.

1. **Merge the umbrella.** `implementation/t81-finish-benchmark` (draft pull request **#39**,
   base `main`) carries every chunk of the programme. The two `marginal` genome-wide cells land
   ≈ 2026-09-09, so they arrive either as a follow-up stamp on the umbrella or, if it is merged
   by then, as a small pull request of their own.
2. **Flip `noise_floor.measured`, or leave it.** It is `false` on both boards in
   `leaderboard/boards.json` and every view compiles `unranked`. `tools/leaderboard.py:300-302`
   shows `true` turns ranking **on** for ranked panels and scopes — that is the experiment-lane
   merge gate, so it is the PI's flip. The floor is measured now (three panels, above); the two
   are simply not wired together. If ranking goes on, the `B` figure of 0.2033
   (`oracle_scaled` 0.2091, `scale_error` 0.0058) has to be read beside every `B_` gap, against
   `AGENTS.md` §7.2's ~0.09 clustered floor and its 0.1195 seed-change shift.
3. **A second figure for the pilot board, or not.** The pilot probe exists and is unpublished. A
   pilot figure would be a separate ranking on its own axis — it cannot share the `eic.19` one —
   so it is a second figure or nothing, not an extra series.
4. **The `marginal` pilot genome-wide fallback, if 58260220_4 times out too.** Two options, both
   needing a decision this programme does not own: **(i)** re-run with `CRPS_APPROX=100`
   `CRPS_SEED=0` — the t56 fair-sampled count arm, priced at roughly half the closed-form cost —
   which **changes the numbers**, makes the count-arm CRPS an estimate that must be quoted as
   one, and leaves this one method incomparable to `knn1`/`knn5`/`avg`, whose landed `store.B_`
   jsons are closed-form; or **(ii)** accept the held-out cell alone and blank the genome-wide
   one. The ~174 h projection is one node's rate extrapolated (`fc20672` against `fc30351` for
   `eic_19`), so 58260220_4 may still come in under the band on a different node.

All 205 rows: 182 done, 14 running, 9 queued.
